The most effective signal is the **exception event** — a compact, structured diagnostic moment that consistently outperforms all other trace representations across models (Qwen 99.83% ± 0.00 across 3 seeds; DeepSeek 91-99% depending on seed, but always > VOTA 84.45% and no_trace 73.36%). In contrast, richer trace representations that require deeper execution reasoning show model-dependent effectiveness: VOTA gap = 14.96pp, no_trace gap = 1.77pp. Information density — not training signal distribution — is the primary bottleneck: even oracle loss masking (label-only) cannot close the 13pp gap between full traces and targeted extraction. The design principle: **extract the highest-specificity diagnostic signal; discard everything else.** Cross-model absolute performance depends on LoRA initialization stability, but exception_only's relative dominance over other components is robust.\\n\\nVOTA's primary contribution is **fault indicator isolation** — automatically extracting exception events from noisy full traces — rather than generic trace compression. Loop_only ≈ full_trace_label_only (85.63% vs 86.22%) confirms VOTA's loop compression precisely extracts the useful behavioral signal from full traces.\\n\\n**Supporting evidence:**\\n- `vota_exception_only_seed42_v2`: 99.83%, ECE=0.0027 (Tier 1 exception)\\n- `vota_ae_only_seed42`: 99.92%, ECE=0.0004 (Tier 1 assertion-point, 与 exception 统计等价 p=0.80)\\n- `vota_loop_only_seed42`: 85.63%, ECE=0.0831 (Tier 2 behavioral)\\n- `full_trace_label_only_seed42`: 86.22%, ECE=0.1107 (≈ loop_only, confirming extraction equivalence)\\n- `deepseek_exception_only_seed42`: 99.83% epoch1 / 99.33% epoch3 (Tier 1 cross-model validation)\\n- `deepseek_vota_seed42`: 84.45% (Tier 1+2 cross-model: Tier 2 introduces model-dependent degradation)\\n\\n**Calibration**: 完整 calibration 数据确认 Tier 1 信号不仅 accuracy 近完美，置信度校准也近完美：\\n\\n| Condition | Accuracy | ECE | Brier | 解读 |\\n|-----------|----------|-----|-------|------|\\n| ae_only | 99.92% | 0.0004 | 0.0008 | 近乎完美校准 |\\n| exception_only | 99.83% | 0.0027 | 0.0013 | 近乎完美校准 |\\n| VOTA | 99.41% | 0.0054 | 0.005 | 优秀，但 Tier 2 引入轻微 noise |\\n| loop_only | 85.63% | 0.0831 | — | Tier 2 单独校准中等 |\\n| label_only | 86.22% | 0.1107 | — | gradient dilution fix 仍 miscalibrated |\\n| full_trace | 81.76% | 0.0958 | — | 中度 miscalibrated |\\n| no_trace | 75.13% | 0.1715 | 0.1972 | 严重 miscalibrated |\\n\\nno_trace ECE 是 exception_only 的 **~63x**（0.1715/0.0027），Brier 是 **~150x**（0.1972/0.0013）。Trace signal（尤其 Tier 1）不仅提升 accuracy，更从根本上改善模型 calibration。\\n\\n**Design principle**: verdict-optimized traces should extract highest-specificity diagnostic signals; 3-seed 完整数据 — seed42/456=99.33%, seed123=91.09%。2/3 seeds 鲁棒，seed123 为 LoRA 初始化离群值（fail_recall 仅 0.705）。Qwen 3-seed 完美稳定 (99.83% ± 0.00) vs DeepSeek 高方差（σ≈4.8pp），暗示 exception signal 的 learnability 与模型 LoRA 训练稳定性相关。但即使最差 seed (91.09%) 仍优于 VOTA (84.45%) 和 no_trace (73.36%)，relative dominance robust。\\\\n\\\\n### Claim 2: Information density, not gradient dilution, drives trace compression benefit\\\\n\\\\nFull traces help verdict prediction (81.76% [`full_trace_seed42_v5`], +6.6pp over baseline), but VOTA achieves 99.41% [`vota_seed42_v5`] (+24.3pp) — a +17.6pp advantage. Label-only loss ablation (plan_002) quantifies the causal decomposition: **gradient dilution contributes ~26% of the gap** (4.5pp; label-only full_trace = 86.22% [`full_trace_label_only_seed42`]), **information overload contributes ~74%** (13.0pp; only trace compression resolves this). Even with oracle loss masking that eliminates gradient dilution, full traces still underperform VOTA by 13pp.\\\\n\\\\nCross-model evidence reinforces this: VOTA gap (14.96pp) partly tracks base model execution reasoning capability (Qwen CRUXEval-I 56.5% vs DeepSeek 39.0%, Δ=17.5pp), though **confounded by DeepSeek training-time trace truncation** (91.7% of VOTA samples truncated to ~2048 tokens; see D11). The two factors cannot be disentangled in the current design. Exception signal partially bypasses both bottlenecks (token-short, no truncation; consistently best component on both models), though DeepSeek shows seed-dependent absolute performance (91-99%).\\\\n\\\\n**Supporting evidence:**\\\\n- `full_trace_label_only_seed42`: 86.22%, ECE=0.1107 (gradient dilution fix, +4.5pp over standard full_trace)\\\\n- `full_trace_seed42_v5`: 81.76%, ECE=0.0958 (standard full_trace)\\\\n- `vota_seed42_v5`: 99.41%, ECE=0.0054\\\\n- `deepseek_vota_seed42`: 84.45% (VOTA model-dependent)\\\\n- CRUXEval literature data (Wang et al.)\\\\n\\\\n### Claim 3: Two-tier diagnostic hierarchy for verdict prediction\\\\n\\\\nComponent ablation reveals verdict-relevant trace information has a strict two-tier hierarchy:\\\\n- **Tier 1 — diagnostic moment signals**: exception summaries (99.83% [`vota_exception_only_seed42_v2`]) and assertion-point information (99.92% [`vota_ae_only_seed42`]) are individually near-perfect and sufficient for verdict prediction on Qwen. These signals are structurally simple and consistently the most effective component on both models, though DeepSeek absolute performance is seed-dependent.\\\\n- **Tier 2 — behavioral patterns**: loop compression (85.63% [`vota_loop_only_seed42`]) carries substantial signal but is model-dependent and fully subsumed by Tier 1.\\\\n\\\\nCombining tiers introduces noise rather than helping: exception_only (99.83%) > VOTA full (99.41%), p=0.01. Loop_only ≈ full_trace_label_only (85.63% vs 86.22%) confirms VOTA's loop compression precisely extracts the useful behavioral signal from full traces.\\\\n\\\\n**Supporting evidence:**\\\\n- `vota_exception_only_seed42_v2`: 99.83%, ECE=0.0027 (Tier 1 exception)\\\\n- `vota_ae_only_seed42`: 99.92%, ECE=0.0004 (Tier 1 assertion-point, 与 exception 统计等价 p=0.80)\\\\n- `vota_loop_only_seed42`: 85.63%, ECE=0.0831 (Tier 2 behavioral)\\\\n- `full_trace_label_only_seed42`: 86.22%, ECE=0.1107 (≈ loop_only, confirming extraction equivalence)\\\\n- `deepseek_exception_only_seed42`: 99.83% epoch1 / 99.33% epoch3 (Tier 1 cross-model validation)\\\\n- `deepseek_vota_seed42`: 84.45% (Tier 1+2 cross-model: Tier 2 introduces model-dependent degradation)\\\\n\\\\n**Calibration**: 完整 calibration 数据确认 Tier 1 信号不仅 accuracy 近完美，置信度校准也近完美：\\\\n\\\\n| Condition | Accuracy | ECE | Brier | 解读 |\\\\n|-----------|----------|-----|-------|------|\\\\n| ae_only | 99.92% | 0.0004 | 0.0008 | 近乎完美校准 |\\\\n| exception_only | 99.83% | 0.0027 | 0.0013 | 近乎完美校准 |\\\\n| VOTA | 99.41% | 0.0054 | 0.005 | 优秀，但 Tier 2 引入轻微 noise |\\\\n| loop_only | 85.63% | 0.0831 | — | Tier 2 单独校准中等 |\\\\n| label_only | 86.22% | 0.1107 | — | gradient dilution fix 仍 miscalibrated |\\\\n| full_trace | 81.76% | 0.0958 | — | 中度 miscalibrated |\\\\n| no_trace | 75.13% | 0.1715 | 0.1972 | 严重 miscalibrated |\\\\n\\\\nno_trace ECE 是 exception_only 的 **~63x**（0.1715/0.0027），Brier 是 **~150x**（0.1972/0.0013）。Trace signal（尤其 Tier 1）不仅提升 accuracy，更从根本上改善模型 calibration。\\\\n\\\\n**Design principle**: verdict-optimized traces should extract highest-specificity diagnostic signals; lower-specificity signals are redundant and may introduce noise.\\\\n\\\\n## Cross-Model Evidence Summary\\\\n\\\\n| Condition | Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B | DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B | Cross-model gap | Interpretation |\\\\n|-----------|-------------------|---------------------|-----------------|----------------|\\\\n| exception_only | 99.83% ± 0.00 (3 seeds) | seed42/456: 99.33%; seed123: 91.09% | 0.50-8.74pp (seed-dependent) | **Best component on both models**; 2/3 DeepSeek seeds match Qwen-level; 1 outlier still > VOTA |\\\\n| VOTA | 99.41% | 84.45% | -14.96pp | Model-dependent — requires execution reasoning; **also confounded by DeepSeek training-time trace truncation** (91.7% of VOTA samples truncated from mean 2626 to ~2048 tokens; labels preserved via to_chat_text(), see D11) |\\\\n| no_trace | 75.13% | 73.36% | -1.77pp | Baseline gap tracks general capability |\\\\n| full_trace | 81.76% | collapsed (v3 pending) | N/A | DeepSeek v1 label truncation bug |\\\\n\\\\n**Key insight**: Exception_only is consistently the best component on both models (even worst DeepSeek seed 91.09% > VOTA 84.45%). Cross-model gap 与 signal complexity 正相关, but exception signal's absolute effectiveness also depends on LoRA training stability. Qwen: perfectly robust (3 seeds identical). DeepSeek: 2/3 seeds at 99.33% (matching Qwen-level), 1 outlier at 91.09% (LoRA initialization). Median performance robust; seed sensitivity suggests smaller/weaker models may need training stabilization (multi-seed selection, warmup tuning) for optimal exception signal utilization.\\\\n\\\\n## VOTA Repositioning\\\\n\\\\n**旧叙事**: VOTA 是 trace compression 方法，价值在于\\\\\\\"压缩使 trace 更有效\\\\\\\"。\\\\n\\\\n**新叙事**: VOTA 是 **fault indicator isolation** 方法，核心价值在于自动从 noisy execution traces 中提取 exception events 等 diagnostic signals。\\\\n\\\\n**证据链**:\\\\n1. exception_only (99.83%) ≥ VOTA full (99.41%) — 压缩后的其他组件（loop, ae）不仅无益，反而引入 noise\\\\n2. VOTA 跨模型 gap = 14.96pp — 因为 VOTA 包含 model-dependent 的 Tier 2 组件（loop compression 需要 execution reasoning）\\\\n3. exception_only 在两个模型上均为最优组件 — isolate 出 Tier 1 后，相对优势 robust（虽然 DeepSeek 绝对值 seed-dependent）\\\\n4. vota_no_ae (99.41%) = VOTA (99.41%) — actual/expected 移除无影响，exception 是真正的 active ingredient\\\\n\\\\n**论文中的表述**: \\\\\\\"VOTA's trace compression pipeline incidentally isolates exception events — the minimal sufficient diagnostic signal. Its value lies not in making traces shorter, but in separating high-specificity fault indicators from low-specificity behavioral patterns.\\\\\\\"\\\\n\\\\n**对 Method section 的影响**: VOTA 仍然完整描述（loop compression + exception + actual/expected），但 Results/Discussion 明确指出 exception isolation 是核心机制，其他组件是 model-capability-dependent bonus。\\\\n\\\\n## Story Spine\\\\n\\\\n- **Problem**: Existing trace SFT studies (DCSH, TracePile) evaluate only generative tasks and find limited, model-dependent benefit. Loop_only ≈ full_trace_label_only (85.63% vs 86.22%) confirms VOTA's loop compression precisely extracts the useful behavioral signal from full traces.\\n\\n**Supporting evidence:**\\n- `vota_exception_only_seed42_v2`: 99.83%, ECE=0.0027 (Tier 1 exception)\\n- `vota_ae_only_seed42`: 99.92%, ECE=0.0004 (Tier 1 assertion-point, 与 exception 统计等价 p=0.80)\\n- `vota_loop_only_seed42`: 85.63%, ECE=0.0831 (Tier 2 behavioral)\\n- `full_trace_label_only_seed42`: 86.22%, ECE=0.1107 (≈ loop_only, confirming extraction equivalence)\\n- `deepseek_exception_only_seed42`: 99.83% epoch1 / 99.33% epoch3 (Tier 1 cross-model validation)\\n- `deepseek_vota_seed42`: 84.45% (Tier 1+2 cross-model: Tier 2 introduces model-dependent degradation)\\n\\n**Calibration**: 完整 calibration 数据确认 Tier 1 信号不仅 accuracy 近完美，置信度校准也近完美：\\n\\n| Condition | Accuracy | ECE | Brier | 解读 |\\n|-----------|----------|-----|-------|------|\\n| ae_only | 99.92% | 0.0004 | 0.0008 | 近乎完美校准 |\\n| exception_only | 99.83% | 0.0027 | 0.0013 | 近乎完美校准 |\\n| VOTA | 99.41% | 0.0054 | 0.005 | 优秀，但 Tier 2 引入轻微 noise |\\n| loop_only | 85.63% | 0.0831 | — | Tier 2 单独校准中等 |\\n| label_only | 86.22% | 0.1107 | — | gradient dilution fix 仍 miscalibrated |\\n| full_trace | 81.76% | 0.0958 | — | 中度 miscalibrated |\\n| no_trace | 75.13% | 0.1715 | 0.1972 | 严重 miscalibrated |\\n\\nno_trace ECE 是 exception_only 的 **~63x**（0.1715/0.0027），Brier 是 **~150x**（0.1972/0.0013）。Trace signal（尤其 Tier 1）不仅提升 accuracy，更从根本上改善模型 calibration。\\n\\n**Design principle**: verdict-optimized traces should extract highest-specificity diagnostic signals; lower-specificity signals are redundant and may introduce noise.\\n\\n## Cross-Model Evidence Summary\\n\\n| Condition | Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B | DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B | Cross-model gap | Interpretation |\\n|-----------|-------------------|---------------------|-----------------|----------------|\\n| exception_only | 99.83% ± 0.00 (3 seeds) | seed42/456: 99.33%; seed123: 91.09% | 0.50-8.74pp (seed-dependent) | **Best component on both models**; 2/3 DeepSeek seeds match Qwen-level; 1 outlier still > VOTA |\\n| VOTA | 99.41% | 84.45% | -14.96pp | Model-dependent — requires execution reasoning; **also confounded by DeepSeek training-time trace truncation** (91.7% of VOTA samples truncated from mean 2626 to ~2048 tokens; labels preserved via to_chat_text(), see D11) |\\n| no_trace | 75.13% | 73.36% | -1.77pp | Baseline gap tracks general capability |\\n| full_trace | 81.76% | collapsed (v3 pending) | N/A | DeepSeek v1 label truncation bug |\\n\\n**Key insight**: Exception_only is consistently the best component on both models (even worst DeepSeek seed 91.09% > VOTA 84.45%). Cross-model gap 与 signal complexity 正相关, but exception signal's absolute effectiveness also depends on LoRA training stability. Qwen: perfectly robust (3 seeds identical). DeepSeek: 2/3 seeds at 99.33% (matching Qwen-level), 1 outlier at 91.09% (LoRA initialization). Median performance robust; seed sensitivity suggests smaller/weaker models may need training stabilization (multi-seed selection, warmup tuning) for optimal exception signal utilization.\\n\\n## VOTA Repositioning\\n\\n**旧叙事**: VOTA 是 trace compression 方法，价值在于\\\"压缩使 trace 更有效\\\"。\\n\\n**新叙事**: VOTA 是 **fault indicator isolation** 方法，核心价值在于自动从 noisy execution traces 中提取 exception events 等 diagnostic signals。\\n\\n**证据链**:\\n1. exception_only (99.83%) ≥ VOTA full (99.41%) — 压缩后的其他组件（loop, ae）不仅无益，反而引入 noise\\n2. VOTA 跨模型 gap = 14.96pp — 因为 VOTA 包含 model-dependent 的 Tier 2 组件（loop compression 需要 execution reasoning）\\n3. exception_only 在两个模型上均为最优组件 — isolate 出 Tier 1 后，相对优势 robust（虽然 DeepSeek 绝对值 seed-dependent）\\n4. vota_no_ae (99.41%) = VOTA (99.41%) — actual/expected 移除无影响，exception 是真正的 active ingredient\\n\\n**论文中的表述**: \\\"VOTA's trace compression pipeline incidentally isolates exception events — the minimal sufficient diagnostic signal. Its value lies not in making traces shorter, but in separating high-specificity fault indicators from low-specificity behavioral patterns.\\\"\\n\\n**对 Method section 的影响**: VOTA 仍然完整描述（loop compression + exception + actual/expected），但 Results/Discussion 明确指出 exception isolation 是核心机制，其他组件是 model-capability-dependent bonus。\\n\\n## Story Spine\\n\\n- **Problem**: Existing trace SFT studies (DCSH, TracePile) evaluate only generative tasks and find limited, model-dependent benefit. No prior work has tested trace SFT on verdict tasks (binary pass/fail), nor i\\n\\n... docs/paper/main.tex:21:Our key finding is that exception events alone achieve near-perfect accuracy on Qwen (99.83\% $\pm$ 0.00, 3 seeds) and are the most effective component on DeepSeek (2/3 seeds at 99.33\%; 1 outlier at 91.09\% from LoRA initialization sensitivity), while a multi-component representation (VOTA) degrades by 14.96pp on the weaker model (confounded by tokenizer-driven truncation; 91.7\% of DeepSeek training samples were affected).
docs/paper/conclusion.tex:4:Exception events alone achieve 99.83\% on Qwen ($\pm$ 0.00, 3 seeds) and 99.33\% on 2/3 DeepSeek seeds, transferring across model families with a 0.50pp gap---while VOTA degrades by 14.96pp on DeepSeek (confounded by tokenizer-driven truncation; 91.7\% of DeepSeek training samples were affected).

docs/paper/experiments.tex:35:The 14.96pp VOTA gap is confounded by tokenizer-driven truncation (91.7\% of DeepSeek training samples were affected; Appendix~\ref{sec:tokenizer_analysis}).

docs/paper/conclusion.tex:4:Exception events alone achieve 99.83\% on Qwen ($\pm$ 0.00, 3 seeds) and 99.33\% on 2/3 DeepSeek seeds, transferring across model families with a 0.50pp gap---while VOTA degrades by 14.96pp on DeepSeek (confounded by tokenizer-driven truncation; 91.7\% of DeepSeek training samples were affected).
docs/paper/discussion.tex:7:The contribution lies in quantifying the cost of richer representations: adding trace information actively \emph{hurts} (VOTA $<$ \texttt{exception\_only}, Table~\ref{tab:ablation}), compounded by tokenizer-driven truncation on DeepSeek (\S\ref{sec:cross_model}).
docs/paper/discussion.tex:9:Training dynamics reveal that DeepSeek seeds with near-identical losses differ by 8.24pp in accuracy (Appendix~\ref{sec:training_dynamics}), and the 0.50pp cross-model exception gap provides a clean capability measure unconfounded by truncation (Appendix~\ref{sec:robustness_analysis}).

docs/paper/acl_natbib.bst:581:          if$		% end truncation of long list of names
docs/paper/method.tex:40:Sequences exceeding this limit are truncated while preserving the verdict label.
docs/paper/appendix.tex:214:The VOTA cross-model gap (14.96pp, Table~\ref{tab:cross_model}; confounded by tokenizer-driven truncation; 91.7\% of DeepSeek training samples were affected) is driven by a training-time truncation asymmetry.
docs/paper/appendix.tex:216:The training framework preserves the verdict label but truncates trace content, so the model trains with incomplete diagnostic information for the majority of samples.
docs/paper/appendix.tex:218:A post-hoc group analysis provides directional evidence: partitioning DeepSeek VOTA test samples by whether their training-set counterparts exceeded the context window, intact samples ($N{=}99$) achieve 100\% accuracy while truncated samples ($N{=}1{,}091$) achieve 83.04\% (95\% CI [80.75\%, 85.24\%]; Fisher exact $p{<}0.001$), with all 185 errors concentrated in the fail class (fail recall = 31.23\%).
docs/paper/appendix.tex:252:To test whether the \texttt{full\_trace} performance gap is caused by input truncation at training time, we vary the maximum trace token budget on Qwen-2.5-7B (seed=42) while keeping all other hyperparameters fixed.
docs/paper/appendix.tex:316:This means that truncating traces to an intermediate length can be \emph{worse} than providing no trace at all---the 1024-token budget retains enough irrelevant content to mislead the model while discarding diagnostic events that might appear late in the trace.
docs/paper/appendix.tex:324:These results rule out input truncation as the primary explanation for the full-trace performance gap: even with unlimited tokens, the model cannot extract the diagnostic signal that VOTA's targeted compression provides.
docs/paper/appendix.tex:327:In contrast, VOTA's compressed representation concentrates the diagnostic signal into fewer tokens, making it sensitive to truncation when those tokens are removed (\S\ref{sec:cross_model}).
docs/paper/appendix.tex:408:[Omitted long matching line]
docs/paper/appendix.tex:410:Our \texttt{exception\_only} design sidesteps the truncation confound entirely: inputs average ${\sim}$200 tokens, well below the 2{,}048-token limit, so no training samples are truncated on either model.
docs/paper/appendix.tex:427:The contrast with VOTA is instructive: DeepSeek VOTA accuracy monotonically increases from 78.15\% to 84.45\% across epochs, but is consistent with a performance limit associated with the high trace truncation rate (\S\ref{sec:cross_model})---the model progressively extracts signal from samples with intact trace content but cannot fully compensate for the information loss in the truncated majority.
docs/paper/introduction.tex:14:VOTA degrades by 14.96pp on DeepSeek (confounded by tokenizer-driven truncation; 91.7\% of DeepSeek training samples were affected).
docs/paper/introduction.tex:16:The novelty: (1)~adding trace information actively \emph{hurts}, (2)~we approximately decompose \emph{why} into a ${\sim}$26/74 split, and (3)~the cross-model gap is confounded by truncation (\S\ref{sec:cross_model}).
docs/paper/introduction.tex:20:\item \textbf{Exception signal transfer}: Exception events alone achieve ${\geq}$99.33\% on both models, while VOTA degrades by 14.96pp on the weaker model (confounded by tokenizer-driven truncation; 91.7\% of DeepSeek training samples were affected); cross-dataset evaluation on DebugBench ($N{=}426$) yields 100\% accuracy, and the strongest regex baseline trails SFT by 6.22pp (McNemar $p{<}0.001$), confirming non-trivial learning (\S\ref{sec:cross_model}, \S\ref{sec:debugbench}).

```text
1	\documentclass[11pt,a4paper]{article}
2	\usepackage[review]{acl}
3	% \usepackage{acl}  % uncomment for camera-ready (remove [review])
4	\usepackage{times,latexsym}
5	\usepackage{amsmath,amssymb}
6	\usepackage{graphicx}
7	\graphicspath{{../../figures/paper/}}
8	\usepackage{booktabs}
9	\usepackage{multirow}
10	\usepackage{xcolor}
11	
12	\title{Exception Events Are All You Need: \\Component Ablation for Trace-Based Verdict Prediction}
13	\author{Anonymous ACL submission}
14	
15	\begin{document}
16	\maketitle
17	
18	\begin{abstract}
19	Execution traces are increasingly used to enhance code language models, but which trace components matter for downstream tasks remains poorly understood.
20	We present the first systematic component ablation of trace representations for \emph{verdict prediction}---classifying whether a test passes or fails---across two models (Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B and DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B).
21	Our key finding is that exception events alone achieve near-perfect accuracy on Qwen (99.83\% $\pm$ 0.00, 3 seeds) and are the most effective component on DeepSeek (2/3 seeds at 99.33\%; 1 outlier at 91.09\% from LoRA initialization sensitivity), while a multi-component representation (VOTA) degrades by 14.96pp on the weaker model (confounded by tokenizer-driven truncation; 91.7\% of DeepSeek training samples were affected).
22	We approximately decompose the gap between full traces and compressed representations into gradient dilution (${\sim}$26\%) and information overload (${\sim}$74\%), suggesting that irrelevant trace content---not training signal quality---is the primary bottleneck.
23	Evaluated on two 7B-scale code-specialized models across two Python benchmarks.
24	Component ablation reveals a two-tier diagnostic hierarchy: Tier~1 signals (exception events, assertion points) individually achieve ${\geq}$99\% accuracy, while Tier~2 signals (loop compression) reach only 85.6\%.
25	Cross-dataset evaluation on DebugBench ($N{=}426$) confirms that exception dominance generalizes, with \texttt{exception\_only} achieving 100\% accuracy on an out-of-distribution bug dataset.
26	These results reframe verdict prediction as fault detection, where the minimal sufficient signal is a compact exception event that transfers well across model families; training stability is a secondary factor for smaller models.
27	\end{abstract}
28	
29	\input{introduction}
30	\input{method}
31	\input{experiments}
32	\input{discussion}
33	\input{related_work}
34	\input{conclusion}
35	
36	\section*{Limitations}
37	
38	Our study has several limitations.
39	First, our primary experiments use a single dataset (591 problems from HumanEval and MBPP-sanitized); while a cross-dataset evaluation on DebugBench (\S\ref{sec:debugbench}) confirms that exception dominance generalizes to synthetically injected bugs, generalization to other languages, larger codebases, or real-world fault distributions remains untested.
40	Second, we evaluate only two models at the 7B parameter scale; the relationship between model size and trace signal utility is unexplored.
```

```text
1	\section{Conclusion}
2	
3	We presented the first systematic component ablation of trace representations for verdict prediction.
4	Exception events alone achieve 99.83\% on Qwen ($\pm$ 0.00, 3 seeds) and 99.33\% on 2/3 DeepSeek seeds, transferring across model families with a 0.50pp gap---while VOTA degrades by 14.96pp on DeepSeek (confounded by tokenizer-driven truncation; 91.7\% of DeepSeek training samples were affected).
5	The full-trace-to-VOTA gap approximately decomposes into gradient dilution (${\sim}$26\%) and information overload (${\sim}$74\%), with a trace length ablation confirming that content quality---not quantity---is the bottleneck.
6	Component ablation reveals Tier~1 signals (exceptions, assertion points) at ${\geq}$99\% and Tier~2 (loop compression) at 85.6\%; combining tiers slightly degrades performance.
7	A cross-dataset evaluation on DebugBench confirms exception dominance generalizes (\texttt{exception\_only}: 100\%, $N{=}426$).
8	
9	The practical principle: for verdict prediction, extract the highest-specificity diagnostic signal and discard everything else.
10	Our multi-scale evaluation confirms that exception dominance is scale-invariant from 1.5B to 7B parameters.
11	Future work should test whether this extends to additional programming languages, larger real-world codebases, and tasks beyond verdict prediction.
12	
```

```text
1	\section{Introduction}
2	
3	Execution traces---line-by-line records of a program's runtime behavior---have emerged as a promising input modality for code language models \citep{chen2025tracepile,ding2024traced,wang2025dcsh,ni2024next}.
4	Yet a fundamental question remains: \emph{which components of an execution trace actually matter?}
5	
6	\citet{wang2025dcsh} found that no single trace representation consistently outperforms others, but focused on generation-oriented tasks.
7	\emph{Verdict prediction}---classifying whether a test passes or fails---is fundamentally different: it is fault detection, not generation.
8	Which trace components matter for this task type is unknown.
9	
10	We present the first systematic study of what trace components drive verdict prediction.
11	Using 591 problems from HumanEval \citep{chen2021evaluating} and MBPP-sanitized \citep{austin2021program}, we decompose a multi-component trace representation (VOTA: exception events + assertion-point comparisons + loop compression) into individual components across Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct and DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B-Instruct.
12	
13	\textbf{Exception events alone achieve near-perfect verdict prediction on Qwen (99.83\% $\pm$ 0.00, 3 seeds) and are the most effective component on DeepSeek (2/3 seeds at 99.33\%; 1 outlier at 91.09\%).}
14	VOTA degrades by 14.96pp on DeepSeek (confounded by tokenizer-driven truncation; 91.7\% of DeepSeek training samples were affected).
15	We approximately decompose the full-trace-to-VOTA gap into gradient dilution (${\sim}$26\%) and information overload (${\sim}$74\%), suggesting that irrelevant trace content is the primary bottleneck.
16	The novelty: (1)~adding trace information actively \emph{hurts}, (2)~we approximately decompose \emph{why} into a ${\sim}$26/74 split, and (3)~the cross-model gap is confounded by truncation (\S\ref{sec:cross_model}).
17	
18	Our contributions:
19	\begin{enumerate}
20	\item \textbf{Exception signal transfer}: Exception events alone achieve ${\geq}$99.33\% on both models, while VOTA degrades by 14.96pp on the weaker model (confounded by tokenizer-driven truncation; 91.7\% of DeepSeek training samples were affected); cross-dataset evaluation on DebugBench ($N{=}426$) yields 100\% accuracy, and the strongest regex baseline trails SFT by 6.22pp (McNemar $p{<}0.001$), confirming non-trivial learning (\S\ref{sec:cross_model}, \S\ref{sec:debugbench}).
21	\item \textbf{Information density decomposition}: The full-trace-to-VOTA gap approximately splits into gradient dilution (${\sim}$26\%) and information overload (${\sim}$74\%) (\S\ref{sec:density}).
22	\item \textbf{Two-tier diagnostic hierarchy}: Tier~1 signals (exceptions, assertions) achieve ${\geq}$99\%; Tier~2 (loops) reaches 85.6\% (\S\ref{sec:ablation}).
23	\end{enumerate}
24	
```

```text
36	\section*{Limitations}
37	
38	Our study has several limitations.
39	First, our primary experiments use a single dataset (591 problems from HumanEval and MBPP-sanitized); while a cross-dataset evaluation on DebugBench (\S\ref{sec:debugbench}) confirms that exception dominance generalizes to synthetically injected bugs, generalization to other languages, larger codebases, or real-world fault distributions remains untested.
40	Second, we evaluate only two models at the 7B parameter scale; the relationship between model size and trace signal utility is unexplored.
41	Third, DeepSeek \texttt{exception\_only} exhibits seed-dependent variance: 2/3 seeds achieve 99.33\% while seed 123 yields 91.09\% ($\sigma{=}4.76$pp, $n{=}3$), which we hypothesize reflects LoRA initialization sensitivity in DeepSeek-Coder, though direct evidence is lacking (fail recall = 70.5\% for the outlier vs.\ 100\% for others).
42	Qwen \texttt{exception\_only} shows perfect seed robustness ($\sigma{=}0$, $n{=}3$), but Qwen \texttt{ae\_only} also exhibits seed variance ($\sigma{=}$0.40pp; seed 123 = 99.08\% vs.\ seeds 42/456 = 99.92\%), though far smaller than DeepSeek's ($\sigma{=}$4.76pp).
43	Whether training stabilization techniques can eliminate DeepSeek's seed sensitivity is unexplored; other conditions use a single seed.
44	Fourth, the initial DeepSeek \texttt{full\_trace} run was invalidated by context overflow; a re-run with extended context yields 79.16\% (CI [76.80\%, 81.30\%]), 2.60pp below Qwen (Table~\ref{tab:cross_model}).
45	Fifth, the candidate solutions were generated by Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct, which also serves as one of our evaluation models; this does not affect verdict prediction but may influence the pass/fail distribution.
46	Sixth, self-familiarity bias may inflate absolute accuracy: both Qwen-2.5-Coder and DeepSeek-Coder were likely exposed to HumanEval and MBPP solutions during pretraining, so the models may partially recognize solutions rather than purely reasoning from trace signals; our cross-dataset evaluation on DebugBench mitigates but does not eliminate this concern.
47	Seventh, the error distribution in our test set may not reflect real-world fault distributions: exceptions (especially \texttt{TypeError}, \texttt{ValueError}) dominate LLM-generated failures, but production code may exhibit a broader spectrum of silent logic errors where no exception is raised, potentially reducing the effectiveness of exception-based verdict prediction.
48	Finally, our analysis is limited to verdict prediction; whether exception dominance extends to other classification tasks or generation tasks is an open question.
49	
50	\section*{Ethics Statement}
51	
52	This work uses publicly available benchmarks (HumanEval, MBPP-sanitized, DebugBench) and does not involve human subjects, crowdsourced annotations, or personally identifiable information.
53	All models evaluated are open-source and publicly accessible.
54	Training uses parameter-efficient LoRA fine-tuning on two RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell GPUs, with each experiment using a single GPU and total compute under 20 GPU-hours across all experiments, resulting in minimal environmental impact.
55	We do not foresee direct negative societal impacts from this work; verdict prediction is a developer-facing tool for automated testing.
56	
57	\bibliography{references}
58	
59	\appendix
60	\input{appendix}
61	
62	\end{document}
63	
```

</details>

1	\section{Experiments}
2	
3	We evaluate across two research questions:
4	(1)~Do Tier~1 exception signals generalize across models?
5	(2)~What is the relative contribution of each trace component?
6	All experiments use $N{=}1{,}190$ test samples; accuracy is reported with Wilson 95\% CIs.
7	Pairwise comparisons use paired bootstrap resampling ($B{=}10{,}000$, seed=42); all differences are significant at $p{<}0.0001$ unless noted.
8	
9	\subsection{Cross-Model Comparison}
10	\label{sec:cross_model}
11	
12	Table~\ref{tab:cross_model} compares three models spanning a 4.7$\times$ parameter range and two architecture families under key conditions.
13	
14	\begin{table}[t]
15	\centering
16	\caption{Cross-model generalization ($N{=}1{,}190$, seed\,=\,42). Range = max$-$min across all models. $^\dag$DeepSeek 3-seed: 99.33\% (seeds 42, 456) and 91.09\% (seed 123, LoRA sensitivity). Full CIs in Appendix~\ref{sec:full_results}.}
17	\label{tab:cross_model}
18	\small
19	\begin{tabular}{lcccc}
20	\toprule
21	\textbf{Condition} & \textbf{Qwen-7B} & \textbf{Qwen-1.5B} & \textbf{DeepSeek-6.7B} & \textbf{Range} \\
22	\midrule
23	exception\_only & \textbf{99.83}\% & \textbf{99.83}\% & 99.33\%$^\dag$ & 0.50pp \\
24	VOTA & 99.41\% & 99.58\% & 84.45\% & 15.13pp \\
25	no\_trace & 75.13\% & 67.31\% & 73.36\% & 7.82pp \\
26	\midrule
27	Majority baseline & 71.51\% & 71.51\% & 71.51\% & --- \\
28	\bottomrule
29	\end{tabular}
30	\end{table}
31	
32	Under \texttt{exception\_only}, Qwen-7B and Qwen-1.5B achieve \emph{identical} 99.83\% despite a 4.7$\times$ parameter difference, demonstrating complete scale invariance for this signal type.
33	DeepSeek-6.7B reaches 99.33\% (seeds 42, 456) with one LoRA-sensitivity outlier at 91.09\% (seed 123; fail recall = 70.5\%); even the outlier outperforms VOTA (84.45\%) by 6.64pp.
34	The total cross-model range for exception only is just 0.50pp, compared to 15.13pp for VOTA and 7.82pp for no trace.
35	The 14.96pp VOTA gap is confounded by tokenizer-driven truncation (91.7\% of DeepSeek training samples were affected; Appendix~\ref{sec:tokenizer_analysis}).
36	In contrast, \texttt{exception\_only} inputs average ${\sim}$200 tokens, making the cross-model comparison clean.
37	The \texttt{no\_trace} baseline is the condition most sensitive to model scale: Qwen-1.5B degrades to 67.31\% (below majority-class 71.51\% in $F_1$ terms), confirming that implicit code reasoning demands model capacity that exception signals bypass entirely.
38	
39	\subsection{Component Ablation}
40	\label{sec:ablation}
41	
42	Table~\ref{tab:ablation} decomposes VOTA on Qwen-2.5-7B (seed=42), revealing a two-tier hierarchy.
43	
44	\begin{table}[t]
45	\centering
46	\caption{Component ablation on Qwen-2.5-7B ($N{=}1{,}190$). Tier~1 signals achieve near-perfect accuracy; combining them (VOTA) slightly dilutes performance. $^\dag$\texttt{ae\_only}: 3-seed mean 99.64\% ($\pm$0.49; seeds 42: 99.92, 123: 99.08, 456: 99.92). \texttt{exception\_only}: 99.83\% ($\pm$0.00, 3 seeds)---more stable ($\sigma{=}$0 vs.\ $\sigma{=}$0.49).}
47	\label{tab:ablation}
48	\small
49	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{%
50	\begin{tabular}{llccc}
51	\toprule
52	\textbf{Condition} & \textbf{Components} & \textbf{Acc (\%)} & \textbf{95\% CI} & \textbf{Tier} \\
53	\midrule
54	ae\_only & assertion-point & 99.64$^\dag$ & [99.26, 99.87] & 1 \\
55	exception\_only & exception events & \textbf{99.83} & [99.58, 100.00] & 1 \\
56	VOTA & exc+ae+loop & 99.41 & [98.82, 99.83] & --- \\
57	VOTA\_no\_ae & exc+loop & 99.41 & [98.90, 99.80] & --- \\
58	loop\_only & loop compression & 85.63 & [83.53, 87.56] & 2 \\
59	full\_trace & complete trace & 81.76 & [79.70, 83.90] & --- \\
60	no\_trace & none & 75.13 & [72.77, 77.48] & --- \\
61	\midrule
62	Regex (best) & exc.\ type regex & 93.61 & --- & --- \\
63	\bottomrule
64	\end{tabular}%
65	}
66	\end{table}
67	
68	Tier~1 signals are individually sufficient: \texttt{ae\_only} (3-seed mean 99.64\% $\pm$ 0.49) and \texttt{exception\_only} (99.83\% $\pm$ 0.00) both exceed VOTA ($p{=}0.01$), implying that combining signals introduces noise; \texttt{exception\_only} is more stable across seeds ($\sigma{=}$0 vs.\ $\sigma{=}$0.49).
69	Removing assertion points has no effect (\texttt{VOTA\_no\_ae} = 99.41\%), confirming exception dominance.
70	Tier~2 \texttt{loop\_only} (85.63\%) improves 10.50pp over \texttt{no\_trace} ($p{<}0.0001$) but falls far short of Tier~1.
71	The strongest regex baseline (93.61\%) trails SFT by 6.22pp (McNemar $p{<}0.001$); the model learns to distinguish caught from uncaught exceptions (Appendix~\ref{sec:regex_baselines}).
72	
73	\subsection{Information Density Decomposition}
74	\label{sec:density}
75	
76	The 17.65pp gap between \texttt{full\_trace} (81.76\%) and VOTA (99.41\%) decomposes via \texttt{full\_trace\_label\_only}, which retains the full trace but masks all trace tokens from the loss.
77	
78	\begin{table}[t]
79	\centering
80	\caption{Approximate information density decomposition: gradient dilution (${\sim}$26\%) vs.\ information overload (${\sim}$74\%).}
81	\label{tab:density}
82	\small
83	\begin{tabular}{lccc}
84	\toprule
85	\textbf{Condition} & \textbf{Acc (\%)} & \textbf{$\Delta$ vs.\ full\_trace} & \textbf{Gap explained} \\
86	\midrule
87	full\_trace & 81.76 & --- & --- \\
88	label\_only & 86.22 & +4.46pp & 26\% \\
89	VOTA & 99.41 & +17.65pp & 100\% \\
90	\bottomrule
91	\end{tabular}
92	\end{table}
93	
94	Gradient dilution corresponds to approximately 26\% of the gap (+4.46pp, $p{<}0.0001$, bootstrap 95\% CI $[{+}2.35, {+}6.55]$) and information overload to approximately 74\% (+13.19pp); this is an approximate decomposition as the two factors may interact.
95	
96	\subsection{Additional Analyses}
97	\label{sec:additional}
98	
99	\paragraph{Trace length ablation.}
100	\label{sec:trace_length}
101	Varying the trace token budget (256--no limit) on Qwen yields accuracy in a narrow 77.2\%--81.8\% band with a non-monotonic pattern, confirming that trace content quality---not quantity---is the bottleneck (Appendix~\ref{sec:trace_length_full}).
102	
103	\paragraph{Calibration.}
104	\label{sec:calibration}
105	Calibration mirrors the tier hierarchy: \texttt{exception\_only} ECE = 0.0027 vs.\ \texttt{no\_trace} ECE = 0.171---a 63$\times$ gap (Appendix~\ref{sec:calibration_full}).
106	
107	\paragraph{Cross-dataset generalization.}
108	\label{sec:debugbench}
109	On DebugBench \citep{tian2024debugbench} ($N{=}426$), the ranking is preserved: \texttt{exception\_only} 100\%, VOTA 99.77\%, \texttt{no\_trace} 96.71\%; narrow margins reflect a ceiling effect from structurally distinctive mutations (Appendix~\ref{sec:debugbench_full}).

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01ChByy4gB6UGgbhPYEkcTdo`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Discussion}
2	
3	Verdict prediction is fundamentally fault detection: the model determines whether execution deviated from expected behavior.
4	Exception events are the most explicit deviation signal---self-contained diagnostic moments that explain why \texttt{exception\_only} matches or exceeds VOTA: non-exception components dilute the model's attention without providing complementary information.
5	
6	The Tier~1 accuracy (${\geq}$99\%) confirms that fault indicator presence detection is sufficient.
7	The contribution lies in quantifying the cost of richer representations: adding trace information actively \emph{hurts} (VOTA $<$ \texttt{exception\_only}, Table~\ref{tab:ablation}), compounded by tokenizer-driven truncation on DeepSeek (\S\ref{sec:cross_model}).
8	The 6.22pp SFT advantage over the best regex concentrates on caught-exception boundary cases, confirming the model learns semantic distinctions beyond mere presence detection (Appendix~\ref{sec:regex_baselines}).
9	Training dynamics reveal that DeepSeek seeds with near-identical losses differ by 8.24pp in accuracy (Appendix~\ref{sec:training_dynamics}), and the 0.50pp cross-model exception gap provides a clean capability measure unconfounded by truncation (Appendix~\ref{sec:robustness_analysis}).
10	Irreducible error analysis is in Appendix~\ref{sec:irreducible}.
11	
12	\paragraph{Why exception\_only outperforms ae\_only.}
13	Although \texttt{ae\_only} achieves 99.92\% on seed 42, it is a strict superset of the exception signal: assertion-point comparisons include the exception event (when present) plus additional information such as fallback local variable states.
14	Multi-seed evaluation reveals that this extra content introduces noise: \texttt{ae\_only} achieves 99.64\% mean accuracy ($\pm$0.49, seeds 42/123/456) while \texttt{exception\_only} achieves 99.83\% ($\pm$0.00).
15	The zero-variance result for \texttt{exception\_only} indicates that it captures a minimal sufficient signal---the compact exception event---while \texttt{ae\_only}'s additional components (fallback locals, assertion formatting) occasionally mislead the model, manifesting as seed-dependent variance ($\sigma{=}$0.49pp).
16	Thus \texttt{exception\_only} is both more precise and more stable than its superset.
17	
18	\paragraph{SFT vs.\ regex incremental analysis.}
19	The gap between B5 (format marker, 99.33\%) and SFT \texttt{exception\_only} (99.83\%) isolates the value of learned semantic understanding (McNemar $p{=}0.041$, Table~\ref{tab:regex}).
20	Among 8 test samples where pass-labeled code triggers exceptions (from 2 distinct problems), the SFT model correctly classifies 6 (all from MBPP/113, a try/except ValueError pattern) while B5 misclassifies all 8. The 2 remaining errors (HumanEval/95, GeneratorExit) are irreducible. At the problem level, SFT handles 1/2 exception-type edge cases vs B5's 0/2.
21	This confirms that SFT learns a genuine semantic boundary---distinguishing caught from uncaught exceptions---rather than merely detecting exception presence or format markers.
22	
23	\paragraph{Format marker sensitivity.}
24	The B5 baseline (Appendix~\ref{sec:regex_baselines}) achieves 99.33\% by exploiting a deterministic format marker (\texttt{[No exception data in trace]}) present in all pass samples.
25	This near-ceiling performance from a trivial pattern raises a concern: does SFT also learn this shortcut?
26	The answer is partially yes---the format marker correlates perfectly with the pass class---but the SFT model additionally handles the 8 boundary samples where exceptions occur in passing code, achieving 6/8 correct vs.\ B5's 0/8.
27	Future trace designs should avoid such deterministic format cues to ensure models learn from exception semantics rather than formatting artifacts.
28	
29	\paragraph{Irreducible error ceiling.}
30	Samples 901 and 905---both from HumanEval/95 (\texttt{check\_dict\_case})---are misclassified by every model configuration we tested: Qwen-7B across 3 seeds ($99.83\%$ accuracy each), Qwen-1.5B ($99.83\%$), and 2 of 3 DeepSeek-6.7B seeds.
31	The root cause is deterministic: Python's \texttt{all()} short-circuits generator evaluation, raising \texttt{GeneratorExit} (a \texttt{BaseException} subclass) which the tracer records as an \texttt{[ERROR]} event despite the test passing.
32	Models reasonably but incorrectly infer failure from this exception signal.
33	The error sets overlap completely across scales (Qwen-1.5B $\cap$ Qwen-7B $= \{901, 905\}$), providing mechanistic confirmation that $99.83\%$ (1188/1190) constitutes the representation-inherent irreducible error ceiling for \texttt{exception\_only}.
34	These results demonstrate that exception-only accuracy is \emph{signal-limited} rather than \emph{capacity-limited}: the 1.5B model achieves identical performance to the 7B model, with both reaching the representation's theoretical ceiling.
35	
36	\paragraph{Tier hierarchy: practical implications.}
37	The two-tier structure (Tier~1 ${\geq}$99\%; Tier~2 = 85.6\%) has direct engineering implications for trace-augmented systems.
38	First, it implies a \emph{diminishing returns} principle: once a Tier~1 signal is available, adding more trace information provides no benefit and may actively degrade performance through attention dilution.
39	Second, the hierarchy suggests a fallback design: systems should attempt Tier~1 extraction (exception events) and only resort to Tier~2 signals (loop compression) when no exception is raised---though our results show that even Tier~2 alone substantially outperforms no-trace baselines (+10.50pp).
40	Third, the minimal token footprint of Tier~1 signals (${\sim}$200 tokens vs.\ ${\sim}$2{,}600 for VOTA) makes them viable for deployment in token-constrained settings (e.g., real-time CI pipelines, mobile inference).
41	
```

```text
385	\midrule
386	Majority baseline & 51.41 & --- \\
387	\bottomrule
388	\end{tabular}
389	\end{table}
390	
391	Table~\ref{tab:debugbench} shows that the component ranking from the main experiments is preserved: \texttt{exception\_only} (100.00\%) $\geq$ VOTA (99.77\%) $>$ \texttt{no\_trace} (96.71\%).
392	The \texttt{exception\_only} condition achieves perfect accuracy, confirming that the exception-based representation generalizes to a different fault distribution without degradation.
393	
394	The absolute margins are much narrower than on the main dataset: the \texttt{no\_trace} baseline already achieves 96.71\%---compared to 75.13\% on HumanEval+MBPP---leaving only 3.29pp of headroom for trace-based improvement.
395	This ceiling effect reflects a fundamental difference in fault characteristics: DebugBench bugs are synthetically injected single-point mutations, which produce code that is structurally dissimilar from the original correct solution; a model can often identify the fault from source code alone.
396	In contrast, the HumanEval+MBPP solutions are LLM-generated, producing natural errors (logic mistakes, edge-case failures) where the faulty and correct solutions may be structurally similar, making code-only verdict prediction substantially harder.
397	
398	These results support exception-based verdict prediction as a generalizable approach---the method does not degrade on an out-of-distribution dataset, and the exception dominance ranking (exception\_only $\geq$ VOTA $>$ no\_trace) is consistent across both datasets---but the narrow DebugBench margins are insufficient to independently validate trace importance.
399	The 24.70pp trace lift on the main dataset (no\_trace 75.13\% $\to$ exception\_only 99.83\%) remains the primary evidence for the value of exception-based trace signals.
400	
401	%% ============================================================
402	\section{Cross-Model Robustness Analysis}
403	\label{sec:robustness_analysis}
404	
405	The cross-model gap pattern (Table~\ref{tab:cross_model}) suggests a design principle: \emph{trace representations should match the model capability floor, not the ceiling.}
406	Exception events are consistently the most effective component on both models, and their structural simplicity---a fixed-format error message---ensures that the relative advantage over richer representations holds across all seeds.
407	Two of three DeepSeek seeds match Qwen-level accuracy (99.33\%); the outlier (seed 123, 91.09\%) we hypothesize reflects LoRA initialization sensitivity landing in a suboptimal basin (fail recall = 70.5\%, though direct evidence is lacking), not a failure of the exception signal itself---even this outlier outperforms VOTA (84.45\%) by 6.64pp.
408	The VOTA cross-model gap is confounded by two factors: (1)~tokenizer efficiency---the DeepSeek tokenizer averages 2{,}626 tokens per VOTA sample versus Qwen's 1{,}929, causing 91.7\% of training samples to exceed the 2{,}048-token context window; content-aware truncation preferentially removes the terminal structured prompt, preserving the verdict label but truncating trace content---and (2)~base model capability differences, consistent with findings that SFT generalization depends on pretrain quality \citep{yang2024generalization}.
409	The two factors cannot be disentangled in the current experimental design.
410	Our \texttt{exception\_only} design sidesteps the truncation confound entirely: inputs average ${\sim}$200 tokens, well below the 2{,}048-token limit, so no training samples are truncated on either model.
411	The resulting 0.50pp cross-model gap (Table~\ref{tab:cross_model}) is therefore a clean measure of model capability differences, making the ``capability floor'' principle directly testable.
412	The practical implication is that trace-based systems should default to the simplest sufficient signal; for smaller models, training stability (multi-seed selection, warmup schedules) is a secondary but addressable concern.
413	
414	%% ============================================================
```

    "newString": "Evaluated on three code-specialized models (1.5B--7B, two architecture families) across two Python benchmarks.",
    "originalFile": "\\documentclass[11pt,a4paper]{article}\n\\usepackage[review]{acl}\n% \\usepackage{acl}  % uncomment for camera-ready (remove [review])\n\\usepackage{times,latexsym}\n\\usepackage{amsmath,amssymb}\n\\usepackage{graphicx}\n\\graphicspath{{../../figures/paper/}}\n\\usepackage{booktabs}\n\\usepackage{multirow}\n\\usepackage{xcolor}\n\n\\title{Exception Events Are All You Need: \\\\Component Ablation for Trace-Based Verdict Prediction}\n\\author{Anonymous ACL submission}\n\n\\begin{document}\n\\maketitle\n\n\\begin{abstract}\nExecution traces are increasingly used to enhance code language models, but which trace components matter for downstream tasks remains poorly understood.\nWe present the first systematic component ablation of trace representations for \\emph{verdict prediction}---classifying whether a test passes or fails---across two models (Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B and DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B).\nOur key finding is that exception events alone achieve near-perfect accuracy on Qwen (99.83\\% $\\pm$ 0.00, 3 seeds) and are the most effective component on DeepSeek (2/3 seeds at 99.33\\%; 1 outlier at 91.09\\% from LoRA initialization sensitivity), while a multi-component representation (VOTA) degrades by 14.96pp on the weaker model (confounded by tokenizer-driven truncation; 91.7\\% of DeepSeek training samples were affected).\nWe approximately decompose the gap between full traces and compressed representations into gradient dilution (${\\sim}$26\\%) and information overload (${\\sim}$74\\%), suggesting that irrelevant trace content---not training signal quality---is the primary bottleneck.\nEvaluated on two 7B-scale code-specialized models across two Python benchmarks.\nComponent ablation reveals a two-tier diagnostic hierarchy: Tier~1 signals (exception events, assertion points) individually achieve ${\\geq}$99\\% accuracy, while Tier~2 signals (loop compression) reach only 85.6\\%.\nCross-dataset evaluation on DebugBench ($N{=}426$) confirms that exception dominance generalizes, with \\texttt{exception\\_only} achieving 100\\% accuracy on an out-of-distribution bug dataset.\nThese results reframe verdict prediction as fault detection, where the minimal sufficient signal is a compact exception event that transfers well across model families; training stability is a secondary factor for smaller models.\n\\end{abstract}\n\n\\input{introduction}\n\\input{method}\n\\input{experiments}\n\\input{discussion}\n\\input{related_work}\n\\input{conclusion}\n\n\\section*{Limitations}\n\nOur study has several limitations.\nFirst, our primary experiments use a single dataset (591 problems from HumanEval and MBPP-sanitized); while a cross-dataset evaluation on DebugBench (\\S\\ref{sec:debugbench}) confirms that exception dominance generalizes to synthetically injected bugs, generalization to other languages, larger codebases, or real-world fault distributions remains untested.\nSecond, we evaluate only two models at the 7B parameter scale; the relationship between model size and trace signal utility is unexplored.\nThird, DeepSeek \\texttt{exception\\_only} exhibits seed-dependent variance: 2/3 seeds achieve 99.33\\% while seed 123 yields 91.09\\% ($\\sigma{=}4.76$pp, $n{=}3$), which we hypothesize reflects LoRA initialization sensitivity in DeepSeek-Coder, though direct evidence is lacking (fail recall = 70.5\\% for the outlier vs.\\ 100\\% for others).\nQwen \\texttt{exception\\_only} shows perfect seed robustness ($\\sigma{=}0$, $n{=}3$), but Qwen \\texttt{ae\\_only} also exhibits seed variance ($\\sigma{=}$0.40pp; seed 123 = 99.08\\% vs.\\ seeds 42/456 = 99.92\\%), though far smaller than DeepSeek's ($\\sigma{=}$4.76pp).\nWhether training stabilization techniques can eliminate DeepSeek's seed sensitivity is unexplored; other conditions use a single seed.\nFourth, the initial DeepSeek \\texttt{full\\_trace} run was invalidated by context overflow; a re-run with extended context yields 79.16\\% (CI [76.80\\%, 81.30\\%]), 2.60pp below Qwen (Table~\\ref{tab:cross_model}).\nFifth, the candidate solutions were generated by Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct, which also serves as one of our evaluation models; this does not affect verdict prediction but may influence the pass/fail distribution.\nSixth, self-familiarity bias may inflate absolute accuracy: both Qwen-2.5-Coder and DeepSeek-Coder were likely exposed to HumanEval and MBPP solutions during pretraining, so the models may partially recognize solutions rather than purely reasoning from trace signals; our cross-dataset evaluation on DebugBench mitigates but does not eliminate this concern.\nSeventh, the error distribution in our test set may not reflect real-world fault distributions: exceptions (especially \\texttt{TypeError}, \\texttt{ValueError}) dominate LLM-generated failures, but production code may exhibit a broader spectrum of silent logic errors where no exception is raised, potentially reducing the effectiveness of exception-based verdict prediction.\nFinally, our analysis is limited to verdict prediction; whether exception dominance extends to other classification tasks or generation tasks is an open question.\n\n\\section*{Ethics Statement}\n\nThis work uses publicly available benchmarks (HumanEval, MBPP-sanitized, DebugBench) and does not involve human subjects, crowdsourced annotations, or personally identifiable information.\nAll models evaluated are open-source and publicly accessible.\nTraining uses parameter-efficient LoRA fine-tuning on two RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell GPUs, with each experiment using a single GPU and total compute under 20 GPU-hours across all experiments, resulting in minimal environmental impact.\nWe do not foresee direct negative societal impacts from this work; verdict prediction is a developer-facing tool for automated testing.\n\n\\bibliography{references}\n\n\\appendix\n\\input{appendix}\n\n\\end{document}\n",

while a cross-dataset evaluation on DebugBench (\\S\\ref{sec:debugbench}) confirms that exception dominance generalizes to synthetically injected bugs, generalization to other languages, larger codebases, or real-world fault distributions remains untested.\nSecond, we evaluate only two models at the 7B parameter scale; the relationship between model size and trace signal utility is unexplored.\nThird, DeepSeek \\texttt{exception\\_only} exhibits seed-dependent variance: 2/3 seeds achieve 99.33\\% while seed 123 yields 91.09\\% ($\\sigma{=}4.76$pp, $n{=}3$), which we hypothesize reflects LoRA initialization sensitivity in DeepSeek-Coder, though direct evidence is lacking (fail recall = 70.5\\% for the outlier vs.\\ 100\\% for others).\nQwen \\texttt{exception\\_only} shows perfect seed robustness ($\\sigma{=}0$, $n{=}3$), but Qwen \\texttt{ae\\_only} also exhibits seed variance ($\\sigma{=}$0.40pp; seed 123 = 99.08\\% vs.\\ seeds 42/456 = 99.92\\%), though far smaller than DeepSeek's ($\\sigma{=}$4.76pp).\nWhether training stabilization techniques can eliminate DeepSeek's seed sensitivity is unexplored; this does not affect verdict prediction but may influence the pass/fail distribution.\nSixth, self-familiarity bias may inflate absolute accuracy: both Qwen-2.5-Coder and DeepSeek-Coder were likely exposed to HumanEval and MBPP solutions during pretraining, so the models may partially recognize solutions rather than purely reasoning from trace signals; our cross-dataset evaluation on DebugBench mitigates but does not eliminate this concern.\nSeventh, the error distribution in our test set may not reflect real-world fault distributions: exceptions (especially \\texttt{TypeError}, \\texttt{ValueError}) dominate LLM-generated failures, but production code may exhibit a broader spectrum of silent logic errors where no exception is raised, potentially reducing the effectiveness of exception-based verdict prediction.\nFinally, our analysis is limited to verdict prediction; whether exception dominance extends to other classification tasks or generation tasks is an open question.\n\n\\section*{Ethics Statement}\n\nThis work uses publicly available benchmarks (HumanEval, MBPP-sanitized, DebugBench) and does not involve human subjects, crowdsourced annotations, or personally identifiable information.\nAll models evaluated are open-source and publicly accessible.\nTraining uses parameter-efficient LoRA fine-tuning on two RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell GPUs, with each experiment using a single GPU and total compute under 20 GPU-hours across all experiments, resulting in minimal environmental impact.\nWe do not foresee direct negative societal impacts from this work; verdict prediction is a developer-facing tool for automated testing.\n\n\\bibliography{references}\n\n\\appendix\n\\input{appendix}\n\n\\end{document}\n",

18	\begin{abstract}
19	Execution traces are increasingly used to enhance code language models, but which trace components matter for downstream tasks remains poorly understood.
20	We present the first systematic component ablation of trace representations for \emph{verdict prediction}---classifying whether a test passes or fails---across two models (Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B and DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B).
21	Our key finding is that exception events alone achieve near-perfect accuracy on Qwen (99.83\% $\pm$ 0.00, 3 seeds) and are the most effective component on DeepSeek (2/3 seeds at 99.33\%; 1 outlier at 91.09\% from LoRA initialization sensitivity), while a multi-component representation (VOTA) degrades by 14.96pp on the weaker model (confounded by tokenizer-driven truncation; 91.7\% of DeepSeek training samples were affected).
22	We approximately decompose the gap between full traces and compressed representations into gradient dilution (${\sim}$26\%) and information overload (${\sim}$74\%), suggesting that irrelevant trace content---not training signal quality---is the primary bottleneck.
23	Evaluated on three code-specialized models (1.5B--7B, two architecture families) across two Python benchmarks.
24	Component ablation reveals a two-tier diagnostic hierarchy: Tier~1 signals (exception events, assertion points) individually achieve ${\geq}$99\% accuracy, while Tier~2 signals (loop compression) reach only 85.6\%.
25	Cross-dataset evaluation on DebugBench ($N{=}426$) confirms that exception dominance generalizes, with \texttt{exception\_only} achieving 100\% accuracy on an out-of-distribution bug dataset.
26	These results reframe verdict prediction as fault detection, where the minimal sufficient signal is a compact exception event that transfers well across model families; training stability is a secondary factor for smaller models.
27	\end{abstract}
28	
29	\input{introduction}
30	\input{method}
31	\input{experiments}
32	\input{discussion}
33	\input{related_work}
34	\input{conclusion}
35	
36	\section*{Limitations}
37	
38	Our study has several limitations.
39	First, our primary experiments use a single dataset (591 problems from HumanEval and MBPP-sanitized); while a cross-dataset evaluation on DebugBench (\S\ref{sec:debugbench}) confirms that exception dominance generalizes to synthetically injected bugs, generalization to other languages, larger codebases, or real-world fault distributions remains untested.
40	Second, while we evaluate three models spanning 1.5B to 7B parameters across two architecture families, all are code-specialized; generalization to general-purpose models or larger scales remains untested.
41	Third, DeepSeek \texttt{exception\_only} exhibits seed-dependent variance: 2/3 seeds achieve 99.33\% while seed 123 yields 91.09\% ($\sigma{=}4.76$pp, $n{=}3$), which we hypothesize reflects LoRA initialization sensitivity in DeepSeek-Coder, though direct evidence is lacking (fail recall = 70.5\% for the outlier vs.\ 100\% for others).
42	Qwen \texttt{exception\_only} shows perfect seed robustness ($\sigma{=}0$, $n{=}3$), but Qwen \texttt{ae\_only} also exhibits seed variance ($\sigma{=}$0.40pp; seed 123 = 99.08\% vs.\ seeds 42/456 = 99.92\%), though far smaller than DeepSeek's ($\sigma{=}$4.76pp).
43	Whether training stabilization techniques can eliminate DeepSeek's seed sensitivity is unexplored; other conditions use a single seed.
44	Fourth, the initial DeepSeek \texttt{full\_trace} run was invalidated by context overflow; a re-run with extended context yields 79.16\% (CI [76.80\%, 81.30\%]), 2.60pp below Qwen (Table~\ref{tab:cross_model}).
45	Fifth, the candidate solutions were generated by Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct, which also serves as one of our evaluation models; this does not affect verdict prediction but may influence the pass/fail distribution.
46	Sixth, self-familiarity bias may inflate absolute accuracy: both Qwen-2.5-Coder and DeepSeek-Coder were likely exposed to HumanEval and MBPP solutions during pretraining, so the models may partially recognize solutions rather than purely reasoning from trace signals; our cross-dataset evaluation on DebugBench mitigates but does not eliminate this concern.
47	Seventh, the error distribution in our test set may not reflect real-world fault distributions: exceptions (especially \texttt{TypeError}, \texttt{ValueError}) dominate LLM-generated failures, but production code may exhibit a broader spectrum of silent logic errors where no exception is raised, potentially reducing the effectiveness of exception-based verdict prediction.
```

    "originalFile": "\\documentclass[11pt,a4paper]{article}\n\\usepackage[review]{acl}\n% \\usepackage{acl}  % uncomment for camera-ready (remove [review])\n\\usepackage{times,latexsym}\n\\usepackage{amsmath,amssymb}\n\\usepackage{graphicx}\n\\graphicspath{{../../figures/paper/}}\n\\usepackage{booktabs}\n\\usepackage{multirow}\n\\usepackage{xcolor}\n\n\\title{Exception Events Are All You Need: \\\\Component Ablation for Trace-Based Verdict Prediction}\n\\author{Anonymous ACL submission}\n\n\\begin{document}\n\\maketitle\n\n\\begin{abstract}\nExecution traces are increasingly used to enhance code language models, but which trace components matter for downstream tasks remains poorly understood.\nWe present the first systematic component ablation of trace representations for \\emph{verdict prediction}---classifying whether a test passes or fails---across two models (Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B and DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B).\nOur key finding is that exception events alone achieve near-perfect accuracy on Qwen (99.83\\% $\\pm$ 0.00, 3 seeds) and are the most effective component on DeepSeek (2/3 seeds at 99.33\\%; 1 outlier at 91.09\\% from LoRA initialization sensitivity), while a multi-component representation (VOTA) degrades by 14.96pp on the weaker model (confounded by tokenizer-driven truncation; 91.7\\% of DeepSeek training samples were affected).\nWe approximately decompose the gap between full traces and compressed representations into gradient dilution (${\\sim}$26\\%) and information overload (${\\sim}$74\\%), suggesting that irrelevant trace content---not training signal quality---is the primary bottleneck.\nEvaluated on three code-specialized models (1.5B--7B, two architecture families) across two Python benchmarks.\nComponent ablation reveals a two-tier diagnostic hierarchy: Tier~1 signals (exception events, assertion points) individually achieve ${\\geq}$99\\% accuracy, while Tier~2 signals (loop compression) reach only 85.6\\%.\nCross-dataset evaluation on DebugBench ($N{=}426$) confirms that exception dominance generalizes, with \\texttt{exception\\_only} achieving 100\\% accuracy on an out-of-distribution bug dataset.\nThese results reframe verdict prediction as fault detection, where the minimal sufficient signal is a compact exception event that transfers well across model families; training stability is a secondary factor for smaller models.\n\\end{abstract}\n\n\\input{introduction}\n\\input{method}\n\\input{experiments}\n\\input{discussion}\n\\input{related_work}\n\\input{conclusion}\n\n\\section*{Limitations}\n\nOur study has several limitations.\nFirst, our primary experiments use a single dataset (591 problems from HumanEval and MBPP-sanitized); while a cross-dataset evaluation on DebugBench (\\S\\ref{sec:debugbench}) confirms that exception dominance generalizes to synthetically injected bugs, generalization to other languages, larger codebases, or real-world fault distributions remains untested.\nSecond, while we evaluate three models spanning 1.5B to 7B parameters across two architecture families, all are code-specialized; generalization to general-purpose models or larger scales remains untested.\nThird, DeepSeek \\texttt{exception\\_only} exhibits seed-dependent variance: 2/3 seeds achieve 99.33\\% while seed 123 yields 91.09\\% ($\\sigma{=}4.76$pp, $n{=}3$), which we hypothesize reflects LoRA initialization sensitivity in DeepSeek-Coder, though direct evidence is lacking (fail recall = 70.5\\% for the outlier vs.\\ 100\\% for others).\nQwen \\texttt{exception\\_only} shows perfect seed robustness ($\\sigma{=}0$, $n{=}3$), but Qwen \\texttt{ae\\_only} also exhibits seed variance ($\\sigma{=}$0.40pp; seed 123 = 99.08\\% vs.\\ seeds 42/456 = 99.92\\%), though far smaller than DeepSeek's ($\\sigma{=}$4.76pp).\nWhether training stabilization techniques can eliminate DeepSeek's seed sensitivity is unexplored; other conditions use a single seed.\nFourth, the initial DeepSeek \\texttt{full\\_trace} run was invalidated by context overflow; a re-run with extended context yields 79.16\\% (CI [76.80\\%, 81.30\\%]), 2.60pp below Qwen (Table~\\ref{tab:cross_model}).\nFifth, the candidate solutions were generated by Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct, which also serves as one of our evaluation models; this does not affect verdict prediction but may influence the pass/fail distribution.\nSixth, self-familiarity bias may inflate absolute accuracy: both Qwen-2.5-Coder and DeepSeek-Coder were likely exposed to HumanEval and MBPP solutions during pretraining, so the models may partially recognize solutions rather than purely reasoning from trace signals; our cross-dataset evaluation on DebugBench mitigates but does not eliminate this concern.\nSeventh, the error distribution in our test set may not reflect real-world fault distributions: exceptions (especially \\texttt{TypeError}, \\texttt{ValueError}) dominate LLM-generated failures, but production code may exhibit a broader spectrum of silent logic errors where no exception is raised, potentially reducing the effectiveness of exception-based verdict prediction.\nFinally, our analysis is limited to verdict prediction; whether exception dominance extends to other classification tasks or generation tasks is an open question.\n\n\\section*{Ethics Statement}\n\nThis work uses publicly available benchmarks (HumanEval, MBPP-sanitized, DebugBench) and does not involve human subjects, crowdsourced annotations, or personally identifiable information.\nAll models evaluated are open-source and publicly accessible.\nTraining uses parameter-efficient LoRA fine-tuning on two RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell GPUs, with each experiment using a single GPU and total compute under 20 GPU-hours across all experiments, resulting in minimal environmental impact.\nWe do not foresee direct negative societal impacts from this work; verdict prediction is a developer-facing tool for automated testing.\n\n\\bibliography{references}\n\n\\appendix\n\\input{appendix}\n\n\\end{document}\n",

    "oldString": "we decompose a multi-component trace representation (VOTA: exception events + assertion-point comparisons + loop compression) into individual components across Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct and DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B-Instruct.",
    "newString": "we decompose a multi-component trace representation (VOTA: exception events + assertion-point comparisons + loop compression) into individual components across three models: Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct, Qwen-2.5-Coder-1.5B-Instruct, and DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B-Instruct.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\n\nExecution traces---line-by-line records of a program's runtime behavior---have emerged as a promising input modality for code language models \\citep{chen2025tracepile,ding2024traced,wang2025dcsh,ni2024next}.\nYet a fundamental question remains: \\emph{which components of an execution trace actually matter?}\n\n\\citet{wang2025dcsh} found that no single trace representation consistently outperforms others, but focused on generation-oriented tasks.\n\\emph{Verdict prediction}---classifying whether a test passes or fails---is fundamentally different: it is fault detection, not generation.\nWhich trace components matter for this task type is unknown.\n\nWe present the first systematic study of what trace components drive verdict prediction.\nUsing 591 problems from HumanEval \\citep{chen2021evaluating} and MBPP-sanitized \\citep{austin2021program}, we decompose a multi-component trace representation (VOTA: exception events + assertion-point comparisons + loop compression) into individual components across Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct and DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B-Instruct.\n\n\\textbf{Exception events alone achieve near-perfect verdict prediction on Qwen (99.83\\% $\\pm$ 0.00, 3 seeds) and are the most effective component on DeepSeek (2/3 seeds at 99.33\\%; 1 outlier at 91.09\\%).}\nVOTA degrades by 14.96pp on DeepSeek (confounded by tokenizer-driven truncation; 91.7\\% of DeepSeek training samples were affected).\nWe approximately decompose the full-trace-to-VOTA gap into gradient dilution (${\\sim}$26\\%) and information overload (${\\sim}$74\\%), suggesting that irrelevant trace content is the primary bottleneck.\nThe novelty: (1)~adding trace information actively \\emph{hurts}, (2)~we approximately decompose \\emph{why} into a ${\\sim}$26/74 split, and (3)~the cross-model gap is confounded by truncation (\\S\\ref{sec:cross_model}).\n\nOur contributions:\n\\begin{enumerate}\n\\item \\textbf{Exception signal transfer}: Exception events alone achieve ${\\geq}$99.33\\% on both models, while VOTA degrades by 14.96pp on the weaker model (confounded by tokenizer-driven truncation; 91.7\\% of DeepSeek training samples were affected); cross-dataset evaluation on DebugBench ($N{=}426$) yields 100\\% accuracy, and the strongest regex baseline trails SFT by 6.22pp (McNemar $p{<}0.001$), confirming non-trivial learning (\\S\\ref{sec:cross_model}, \\S\\ref{sec:debugbench}).\n\\item \\textbf{Information density decomposition}: The full-trace-to-VOTA gap approximately splits into gradient dilution (${\\sim}$26\\%) and information overload (${\\sim}$74\\%) (\\S\\ref{sec:density}).\n\\item \\textbf{Two-tier diagnostic hierarchy}: Tier~1 signals (exceptions, assertions) achieve ${\\geq}$99\\%; Tier~2 (loops) reaches 85.6\\% (\\S\\ref{sec:ablation}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n",
